However, the crime photo indicates that the umbrella is lying open at a distance from the victim, not underneath him. This contradicts Phoenix's testimony that Doug fell on top of his umbrella.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 7 indirectly applies because it suggests that if something is obstructed from view, it cannot be seen clearly. Since the Floor Plans indicate that the stand is blocked from view from White's office, it contradicts White's testimony where he claims to have seen the stand and the victim's movements.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

Given the context, we can infer that Acro's inability to look out of the window while lifting the bust indicates a physical limitation or obstruction. Since Acro mentions that he could not exert force on his lower body, it implies that his legs were injured and possibly immobilized, preventing him from moving to the window. This proposition can be applied to the spatial context of the photos taken by Lotta's camera.

Since both photos were taken automatically and the camera was set up to detect loud noises, the camera's position and orientation should not have changed between these times.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 6 does apply. The contradiction arises because the camera was set up to face the lake and take pictures based on sound detection. If the camera was set up correctly and did not move, it should have captured the same scene in both photos, but it did not. The second photo shows an empty lake, while the first photo shows a shooting incident. **Step 2:** Check if any spatial propositions from the list above apply to this contradiction

This proposition can be applied here to analyze the spatial relationship between Fawles and Valerie.

This means that Valerie was not directly in front of Fawles, but rather to his right side, facing him. Therefore, Fawles's testimony stating that Valerie stood in front of him contradicts the spatial arrangement shown in the photo.

**Step 4:** Explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

The spatial contradiction arises because Fawles's testimony places Valerie directly in front of him, whereas the photo in Evidence 6 clearly shows Valerie positioned to the right of Fawles, facing him. This positioning does not align with Fawles's claim that Valerie was in front of him.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Propositions 13 and 14 are relevant here:
- Prop 13: If a person is shot from the right side then the bullet comes from the right side.
- Prop 14: If a person is shot from the left side then the bullet comes from the left side.

These propositions suggest that the direction from which a person is shot determines the side of the body where the bullet enters. Since Stickler claims the victim was shot from the front, the bullet would not have entered the right temple unless the shooter was positioned to the right of the victim.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the propositions do apply. Stickler's testimony contradicts the autopsy report because if the victim was shot from the front, the bullet would likely enter the forehead or center of the face, not the right temple. This implies that the shooter was not directly in front but rather to the right of the victim.

------------------------------
## INPUT PROVIDED:

- A list of characters, evidences and testimonies
- A set of relevant spatial propositions

## RELEVANT SPATIAL PROPOSITIONS:
Prop 1: If a person is hit from behind in a room, they fall forward in that room
Prop 2: If a person is shot downward in a room, the bullet trajectory goes from high to low in that room
Prop 3: If a person is shot from the front in a room, they fall backward in that room
Prop 4: If a person is covered by an object and that object is behind another object in a room, the person is behind the other object in that room
Prop 5: If a person is covered by an object and that object is far from another object in a room, the person is far from the other object in that room
Prop 6: If a person is in the same room as another person, they can potentially see each other

Prop 8: If a person is locked in a room, they cannot be passed through by anyone else
Prop 9: If a person is open in a room, they can be passed through by others

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, we know that the game was taking place at the Borscht Bowl Club and that the defendant lost the final hand. According to Testimony 5, the defendant grabbed the bottle from the table after losing the final hand. Evidence 2, "Crime Photo 1," shows the victim slumped in a chair and leaning back, suggesting he was hit from the front.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We can use Proposition 1: "If a person is hit from the front then that person falls backward."

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if a person is hit from the front, they will fall backward. This aligns with the description in Evidence 2 where the victim is found leaning back in the chair. However, if the defendant was in front of the victim (since he grabbed the bottle from the table), this would imply that the defendant hit the victim from the front. But, given the description of the victim's position, it seems more likely that the attacker was behind the victim, as per Proposition 2: "If a person is hit from behind then that person falls forward." This creates a contradiction because the spatial arrangement implied by the testimonies does not match the spatial outcome described in the evidence.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 1 applies directly to the contradiction. The spatial arrangement suggested by the testimonies (defendant grabbing the bottle from the table, implying he was in front of the victim) contradicts the spatial outcome shown in the evidence (victim leaning back, suggesting he was hit from the front).

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 2, "testimony": 5}

PARSED RESULT:
--------------------
{
  "evidence": 2,
  "testimony": 5
}

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

None of the listed propositions directly address the contradiction between the action described by Olga and the observed position of the victim. However, we can use spatial reasoning to deduce that if Wright hit Smith with the bottle, and Smith fell backward, it would imply that Wright was in front of Smith. Yet, the description of the crime scene and the positioning of the objects suggest that the bottle could have been used from a distance or in a manner inconsistent with Olga's testimony.

According to Cosmos' testimony, the shooting happened from within the Control Room to the east, which would mean the bullet came from the west direction relative to the Control Room. ## OBJECTIVE:

**You must only answer ONE PAIR.**

## ANALYSIS FRAMEWORK:
Please follow this format:

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

---

## CRITICAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT:
**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, L'Belle states that he was in the hallway, hiding in the shadows, and then fled down the hallway to his right, which had a phone at the end. According to Evidence 5, the Crime Scene Diagram, the hallway leading to the phone is to the south, which connects to the foyer. This implies that L'Belle was fleeing towards the south, into the foyer.

However, Evidence 12, Jinxie's Statement, mentions that she saw the demon flee toward the foyer. If L'Belle was fleeing toward the foyer, and he claims to have been hiding in the shadows, this creates a potential contradiction based on visibility and position.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Propositions 1 and 13 seem relevant here as they deal with visibility and being hidden.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 1 states that if a person is hiding in the shadows of a location and another person is to the right of that location, the first person is not visible to the second person. Proposition 13 states that if a person is inside a room and the room is blocked by a wall, the person cannot be seen from outside the room.

Given these propositions, if L'Belle was indeed hiding in the shadows of the hallway, he would not be visible to Jinxie, who was in the foyer. However, Jinxie's statement suggests she saw the demon (which could be L'Belle) fleeing toward the foyer, implying that L'Belle was visible to her.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the propositions do apply, and they help us understand that L'Belle's claim of being hidden in the shadows contradicts Jinxie's statement of seeing him flee toward the foyer.

**Step 5:** **Provide your answer in JSON format**

{"evidence": 12, "testimony": 0}

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Iris describes a sequence of events involving Maya and Elise. Specifically, Testimony 2 states that "Mystic Elise was the one on the defensive, with her back to the stone lantern." This suggests that Elise was facing away from the stone lantern when she was stabbed. 

On the other hand, Evidence 11 mentions that the "Kurain Master's Talisman" was found in the garden of the Inner Temple. This does not directly relate to the position of Elise relative to the stone lantern, but it provides context about the location of items and possibly the movement of characters within the temple grounds.

However, the key contradiction arises when we consider the spatial relationship between Elise and the stone lantern, and the nature of the wound described in Evidence 11. According to Evidence 11, Elise's cause of death was "loss of blood from stab in back," which aligns with the idea that she had her back to the stone lantern. Yet, the autopsy report (Evidence 11) also indicates that "Body fell 10 ft. after death," suggesting that Elise was stabbed and then fell off the bridge. This implies that she was facing the bridge when she was stabbed, not the stone lantern.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

We need to check if any of the spatial propositions can help us identify the contradiction. Proposition 10 states, "If a person is in a different room from a weapon then the person cannot directly handle the weapon." However, this proposition does not directly apply to the contradiction identified.

Instead, Proposition 5, "If a person is hit from behind then the person will fall towards the direction they were facing," and Proposition 6, "If a person is hit from the front then the person will fall away from the direction they were facing," are relevant.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

According to Proposition 5, if Elise was stabbed from behind (as suggested by the wound in her back), she would fall towards the direction she was facing. Since the autopsy report states that she fell 10 feet after being stabbed, this means she was facing the bridge when she was stabbed, not the stone lantern.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 5 applies directly to the contradiction. Iris's testimony places Elise with her back to the stone lantern, implying she was facing away from the attacker. However, the autopsy report indicates that she was stabbed in the back and fell off the bridge, meaning she must have been facing the bridge when she was stabbed.

**Your answer must end with a JSON format like so:** {"evidence": 2, "testimony": 3}

**The answer JSON should strictly be the last line, followed by nothing else.**

LLM OUTPUT (RAW RESPONSE):
------------------------------
**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Atishon states that Dr. Buff was standing in front of the bookshelves and was struck on the head from behind (Testimony 2 and Testimony 3). This implies that the attacker was positioned behind Dr. Buff relative to the bookshelves. 

However, Evidence 3 shows that the victim is lying in the study next to floor-to-ceiling windows and is covered by books. According to Prop 1, if a person is hit from the front with an object, they would fall backward. Since Dr. Buff was struck from behind, he would likely fall forward, towards the bookshelves or the window, rather than being covered by books.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Propositions 1, 3, and 14 are relevant here. Specifically, Prop 1 states that if a person is hit from the front with an object, then the person falls backward. This proposition can be applied inversely to understand the expected direction of fall when hit from behind.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Prop 1 suggests that if a person is hit from the front, they fall backward. By inverse logic, if a person is hit from behind, they would fall forward. Given that Atishon testified that Datz attacked Dr. Buff from behind (Testimony 2 and Testimony 3), Dr. Buff should have fallen forward, towards the bookshelves or the window. However, Evidence 3 indicates that Dr. Buff was covered by books, suggesting he fell backward, which contradicts the expected outcome based on the spatial proposition.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, the listed propositions do apply, specifically Prop 1.

However, Evidence 2 mentions that the victim's face was partly obscured by an old, crooked hat, suggesting that not everything inside the carriage was visible to the witnesses.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 11 states: "If a person blocks the view of another object then that object cannot be seen." In this context, the old, crooked hat (an object) partially blocked the view of the victim's face, meaning that not everything inside the carriage was visible to the witnesses on the roof deck. This directly contradicts Fairplay's statement that everyone would have seen anyone else inside the carriage.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 11 applies and reveals the contradiction. The presence of the hat obscuring the victim's face indicates that the interior of the carriage wasn't entirely visible, thus contradicting Fairplay's claim that no one could have been inside without being seen.

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

From the testimonies, Rimes Marlon states that at about 10:10 AM, he was in the staff room and heard a loud noise from the pool room. He then went to the door to look in and saw coins scattered around, but could not see the captain (Jack Shipley) or the orca (Orla).

From the evidences, Evidence 15 (Security Card) indicates that only the victim (Jack Shipley) and Sasha Buckler had access to the orca pool. This suggests that Rimes Marlon would not have been able to enter the orca pool room to see the coins unless he had a security card or someone let him in.

Additionally, Evidence 3 (Photo of the Body) and Evidence 12 (Shipley's Autopsy Report) indicate that Jack Shipley was found dead in the orca pool, suggesting that he was inside the pool at the time of the incident. However, Rimes claims he could not see the captain or the orca from the door, implying that they were either not visible due to the layout of the room or were not present.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Propositions 1, 6, and 8 are relevant here. Proposition 1 states that if a person is in the same room as another person, then the first person can potentially see the second person. Proposition 6 states that if a person is in a different room from another person, then the first person cannot see the second person. Proposition 8 states that if a person is in front of an object and the object is in front of another object, then the person is in front of the other object.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

Proposition 6 is particularly relevant here. According to Proposition 6, if Rimes Marlon was in the staff room and the orca pool room is a different room, then Rimes could not see the captain or the orca from the door of the orca pool room. This aligns with Rimes's testimony that he could not see the captain or the orca, but it contradicts the fact that he could see the coins scattered around, which implies he had access to the orca pool room.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 6 does apply, revealing the contradiction. Rimes's ability to see the coins scattered around suggests he had access to the orca pool room, which contradicts the evidence that only the victim and Sasha had the security card to enter the orca pool room.

**Step 1:** **Analyze the spatial contradiction** between evidences and testimonies

Detective Gumshoe's testimonies indicate that he was standing guard in front of the Lobby No. 2 door and did not move from that position until he heard the gunshot. However, Evidence 12 (Balloon Piece) suggests that the gunshot heard was actually a balloon popping, and the balloon piece was found on the windowsill of the hallway window. This implies that the gunshot sound could have come from somewhere else in the hallway or another room, which contradicts the idea that Gumshoe was stationary and only heard the gunshot from his position in front of the Lobby No. 2 door.

**Step 2:** **Check if any spatial propositions** from the list above apply to this contradiction

Propositions 1, 3, 7, and 14 seem relevant to the analysis of the spatial contradiction.

**Step 3:** **If applicable propositions exist,** explicitly state which proposition(s) and explain how they reveal the contradiction

- **Proposition 1:** If a person is in the hallway and hears a gunshot from Lobby No. 2, then the gunshot originated from Lobby No. 2.
- **Proposition 3:** If a person is standing guard in front of a door, then they are in the same location as the door.
- **Proposition 7:** If a person is in a different room from another person when a gunshot occurs, then the person cannot see the gunshot happen.
- **Proposition 14:** If a person is in a hallway and a gunshot is heard, but the gunshot was actually a balloon popping in a different room, then the person is disconnected from the actual source of the sound.

**Step 4:** **If none of the listed propositions apply,** explain the spatial reasoning used to identify the contradiction

In this case, Proposition 14 directly applies. According to Prop 14, if Detective Gumshoe was in the hallway and heard a gunshot, but the gunshot was actually a balloon popping in a different room, then he would be disconnected from the actual source of the sound. Since the balloon piece was found on the windowsill of the hallway window, it suggests that the gunshot sound came from within the hallway itself, not from Lobby No. 2. This contradicts Gumshoe's testimony that he was standing guard in front of the Lobby No. 2 door and did not move until he heard the gunshot.
